Details and artifact paths are in `docs/RESULTS.md`.

Per-update threshold noise at the20um differential-pair kT/C level (~100.8uV) causes~24.6% final-fuse spike mismatch and only~41% pixel argmax agreement in a selected three-patch validation replay. A200um pair still gives~15% mismatch and56% agreement for ~2.48m2 raw all-state cap area; an1000um illustration reduces spike mismatch below1% but costs~62m2 in raw pair capacitors. Increasing analog headroom from0.15→0.9V modestly improves the one-stage noisy replay but does not establish injector linearity or PVT operation. The fixed MIM/shared readout is therefore not noise-qualified. Next compare correlated differential state storage and an active low-noise integrator, then rerun task sensitivity and weighted sequence checks before keeping this architecture.

## Full-frame membrane kT/C sensitivity across active PLIFs — 2026-09-26

Historical result, superseded 2026-09-27: the reset-only and combined-mode replay code replaced the subtractively reset membrane with noise, even at zero noise. Its reset-noise spike, pixel and accuracy figures in this paragraph are invalid. Comparator-only figures were unaffected by this particular bug but remain proxy stresses. Use the corrected full-frame subtractive-reset results at the end of this log and the `_corrected_subtractive_reset.json` artifacts.

The PDK open-loop OTA noise calculation is now available alongside reset kT/C. TT/SS/FF 2um input-pair integrated noise is about100/106/95uV RMS over1Hz–100kHz; a20um input pair falls to36uV but loads the state enough to require a1us sample/isolate phase and programmed threshold offset. The prepared 3/6uV state test then decides by5us, but the device noise remains comparable to the larger0.9V-mapped thresholds. Applying36.27uV independently at every active PLIF comparator on three full validation frames reduces mean labeled accuracy37.87→29.58%, with55.3% pixel agreement. It is a conservative mapping of open-loop small-signal noise, but a real analog readout noise risk. Next assess low-noise sampling/correlated readout and analog scaling; no layout until the switched OTA noise is understood.

### Noise-aware analog direction — 2026-09-26

Astra-free continuation: the OTA noise PSD now exports through10MHz. Ideal chop subtraction estimates ~53uV RMS for a1us averaged phase at20um input and ~44uV at100um; widening alone is insufficient, while200um exceeds the PDK model bin. Full-float complete-frame replay with44.25uV per-update noise across all25 populations gives31.4–32.6% labeled accuracy versus49.82% baseline. Deprioritize OTA chopping as final decision circuitry. Next explore correlated differential event-charge accumulation with a slower/narrower-band decision and compare its physical packet/readout noise and area to the floating MIM path; remain analog and full float.

### Sample-isolated readout status — 2026-09-26

The two-stage shared OTA candidate passed prepared3/6uV and a single signed MOS event only with second-stage white transient noise; that is not complete readout-noise qualification. First-OTA output noise can couple through compensation into the stored membrane. A physical closed-loop buffer alternative gives low nominal state disturbance; at4um input width its10ns `pn` screen gives−0.041uV disturbance and0.389uV output-relation error, versus+0.012uV/0.667uV at2um. Closed-loop PDK noise with ideal1us boxcar sampling estimates35–87uV per buffer for4–100um widths and50–123uV differential for independent pairs; sequential correlated sampling at100um still estimates44.0uV. These are small-signal bounds without switch/noise transient or mismatch, but exceed mapped threshold margins. Next compare same-amplifier correlated sampling/autozero and narrow-band event-charge sensing against5ms service and full-float margins. Keep analog/full-float; no QAT or layout.

At100um input width and100fF compensation, the1us differential noise estimate spans43.3–58.7uV across FF/SS at27C and TT at125C; the tested unity-buffer gain remains near1.0. This modest corner spread leaves the readout above the mapped margins across the sampled PDK conditions. The next experiment remains correlated sample/autozero or charge-domain threshold sensing, with transient switch effects included before replay.

A capacitor-only shared differential sampler also trades read kick for kT/C:30um gives47.8uV single-cap kT/C and8.09uV state disturbance;50um gives28.73uV and19.95uV;100um gives14.38uV and42.96uV; the200um transient exceeded its60s bound. The simple sampler is therefore not accepted. Next compare active differential charge buckets and regenerative threshold sensing, preserving full-float weights and analog operation.

2026-09-26: Added an ideal-input SKY130 clocked-latch smoke. A symmetric TT regenerative comparator resolves ±3µV and larger differences to the expected polarity by 5ns after evaluation across three input widths; zero input is intentionally indeterminate. This has no realistic sample network, source loading, noise or mismatch, so it establishes circuit polarity and ideal regeneration only. Next gate is a switched physical-MIM sampling interface and offset/noise characterization.

2026-09-26: The physical-MIM/TG direct input sampler failed the first latch interface check. Switch/reset feedthrough exceeds the3µV target: measured zero-input offsets were +34.9/−2.8/−2.0µV for 5/10/20µm capacitors, and the20µm case misresolved ±3µV. The next bounded experiment uses the existing bottom-plate sequence and a matched dummy/reference to reduce feedthrough before evaluating the latch.

2026-09-26: Bottom-plate sampler plus matched dummy reduced the10µm zero residual to~0.10µV, but also delivered only~0.08µV from a±3µV source. A30µm cap showed~60µV feedthrough and lost one ±3µV decision polarity. The latch follows its immediate input correctly; the sampler transfer/offset is the unresolved issue. Next calibrate sampler-only transfer with physical source capacitance before further latch claims.

2026-09-26: Follow-up sampler calibration exposed scale-dependent results: ±100µV and ±3µV screens disagree, and tighter ngspice tolerances trigger timestep collapse with ideal DC sources. The sampler response is not calibrated; partial transient output is excluded. Next isolate sampler-only transfer, then reintroduce source capacitance and latch loading individually.

2026-09-26: Corrected the bottom-plate switch order. The sampler-only 10µm MIM path now gives approximately−0.998 gain and near-zero offset across ±3/6/100µV, so prior sampler-failure numbers are superseded. The attached latch resolves ±3µV but gives asymmetric results at ±6µV (−6 wrong, +6 correct). Next isolate latch decision threshold and precharge/evaluation timing before adding mismatch/noise.

2026-09-26: The ideal 2µm latch resolves ±2µV and larger inputs by5ns, but the sampler-attached latch still has an asymmetric boundary: ±3µV pass,−6µV fails and+6µV passes. The isolated sampler itself is clean. Next test correlated input autozero and sample-to-evaluate dead time to identify the latch interface offset.

2026-09-27: Found the regenerative latch was active during its supposed precharge phase. A clocked NMOS footer now holds both outputs high until evaluation and resolves ±3/±6µV nominal sampled inputs in3.5–3.7ns. The10µm real/dummy MIM samples still imply≈202µV RMS differential kT/C, far above3µV; nominal logic is not noise-qualified. Next measure a direct or preamplified clocked readout on the physical membrane, including state kick and service cost.

2026-09-27: Direct read of a properly initialized100µm physical membrane confirms the timing tradeoff. A read TG held on through latch regeneration causes≈69.5µV state disturbance. Cutting it0.5–2ns after evaluation preserves nominal ±3/6µV decisions with≤0.05µV disturbance;3ns cutoff gives≈0.76µV disturbance at3µV. The isolated latch node is≈4.07fF and has≈1mV single-node kT/C scale, so the nominal pass cannot justify architecture acceptance. One100µm MIM per30.976m identities is≈0.31m² bare capacitor area. Next bound shared charge-domain preamplification and storage cost.

2026-09-27: Corrected a full-float replay bug: reset-noise injection previously replaced the PLIF's subtractively reset membrane with noise. The full-frame zero-noise control now matches baseline spikes, classes and logits exactly. Corrected independent-pair reset-noise proxies across all25 populations lower selected-frame labeled accuracy from49.82% to30.49% at10µm MIM and35.67% at100µm MIM; a second100µm frame falls48.18%→34.86%. Prior reset-only and combined-mode replay numbers are invalid; comparator-only results are unaffected by this bug but remain proxies. With a2ns read-TG cutoff, opposing deterministic perturbations of2.22mV at the isolated latch input flip the±3µV decisions; this is not noise yield. The analog readout/state-store architecture remains unqualified. Evidence and source filenames are in `docs/RESULTS.md` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_readout_architecture_boundary.json`; next bound a shared active differential charge bucket and physical analog state-storage cost before layout.

2026-09-29: Added an analytical shared-latch service screen and correlated-reset requirement to the readout boundary report. The measured3.705ns nominal decision alone implies23 ideal channels for one visit to all30.976m states in5ms; complete10ns/100ns/1µs/10µs service scenarios imply62/620/6,196/61,952 channels. These omit acquisition, reset and DAG dependencies. For a100µm MIM pair, illustrative3µV and1µV differential reset-noise levels require correlation at least0.9782 and0.9976 from the20.31µV independent-pair proxy; no physical covariance has been measured. Published SKY130, GF180MCU and IHP SG13G2 MIM densities are all around1.5–2fF/µm², so a simple open-PDK switch does not remove the area/noise issue. Keep SKY130 for the next physical reset/readout experiment, with direct-MOS event injection as the provisional baseline; no layout acceptance.

2026-09-29: Ran bounded corrected full-float complete-frame reset-noise probes at3µV RMS on validation records0/1 and1µV on record0, each with three seeds, all25 PLIFs and0.9V candidate headroom. Do not pick a reset-noise limit from two frames; next physical work must demonstrate noise covariance and readout timing before broader task replay.

2026-09-29: Extended the corrected replay to report six-class per-frame IoU and reran the two3µV probes plus their20.31µV physical100µm MIM-pair proxy comparisons. 2026-09-29: Separated initialization noise from post-spike noise to match the PLIF's subtractive reset semantics. With the20.31µV100µm-pair proxy injected only once at record start, mean IoU is0.12120/0.11591 on validation records0/1, close to0.11938/0.11726 when additional noise is injected after each spike. This is software sensitivity, not a physical reset-noise measurement.

2026-09-29: Extended the architecture report with a two-cap SKY130 MIM area versus required reset-correlation sweep. At10µm square, one pair per state occupies≈6,195mm² bare and needs illustrative `rho≥0.999774` to reach3µV differential RMS; at100µm it occupies≈619,520mm² and needs`rho≥0.978189`. A bounded physical20µm MIM/OTA output-baseline-sample screen with−20/0/+20µV prepared differential gives a0.901mV no-event held-output span while a real MOS event produces only≈0.74–0.77mV output change after simulator no-event subtraction. All tested second-OTA comparator outputs remain high. This nominal deterministic circuit does not cancel the initial offset sufficiently; it has no thermal-noise/mismatch proof. Keep layout gated and continue to a different physical initial-reference/state-store topology.

2026-09-29: A finer gate-fraction0.601 physical MIM/OTA screen turns the initial-offset concern into a decision failure. A bounded physical10µm SKY130 MIM/TG/MOS clamp experiment demonstrates that opening the clamp0.2–10ns before a10.5ns MOS event gives≈16.68µV retained event contribution, but opening at onset or1ns after gives≈157µV or3.22mV. 2026-09-29: Extended the370-event physical weighted sequence to TT/27°C and FF/85°C. Using a separately measured ideal gate map at each corner keeps the magnitude error0.03–1.67% across the selected conditions, with≈0.66–0.68mV event-order spread. 2026-09-29: Tested physical analog weight-gate storage at SS/0°C with PDK MIM/TG/MOS and a5ms hold. Directly connecting a sampled10µm gate capacitor to the injector loses≈75% of a408.94µV reference event even after a short hold because of gate-node charge sharing; a100µm pair with10µs programming still has−2.90% event error after5ms and costs≈2,874.56mm² bare across143,728 PLIF weights. Steering current with gates left connected avoids that connection step, but rail-idle drain switching injects≈21.93mV even for an off-gate event. VCM-idle precharge reduces the switch-only term to≈0.351µV, yet the gate-dependent event shifts+91/+20/+3.1/+0.77% after5ms for10/20/50/100µm weight-cap pairs and creates continuous idle current. A short-refresh sweep is nonmonotonic from initial programming offset. Neither route is qualified; next bound a shared buffered gate path or a real low-leak refresh/reference scheme before full-fanin integration. Machine evidence is `sky130_ms_weight_gate_storage_boundary.json`.

2026-09-29: Screened the existing SKY130 OTA as a shared analog weight-gate buffer at SS/0°C. At ideal bias0.65/0.733V, DC follower error is≈−0.024mV at0.936V but−1.246mV at0.48V and−2.672mV at0.30V. With physical injector gate loads, an off-rail-to-weight step lets the p output reach command in≈0.1µs, while the n output does not settle near its final0.47875V until≈7–9µs and overshoots at5µs. The two-OTA plus MOS-load bench draws≈15µA at1.8V. This cannot directly replace ideal gate drivers for50ns-spaced events; a prefetched/shared buffer would need its own mux, calibration, noise and service proof. Architecture boundary JSON updated; no storage or layout acceptance.

2026-09-30: Extended the 12-bit TT-trimmed R-2R injector test to repeated signed events. Ten TT/27°C same-sign pulses at 50ns spacing preserve the single-event transfer deficit: −2.131% at fraction0.5/geometry4 and −1.415% at0.8/geometry2. Negative ten-event trains mirror the positive response. Five-event trains using the same TT codes yield −3.440/−3.577% at SS/0°C and −1.916/−1.002% at FF/27°C. The ten-event SS run hit timestep collapse on a later pulse; no data from it is used. Repeated transfer is stable at these two gate points, but the DAC has ideal resistors and lacks noise, mismatch, extraction, real float weight mapping and readout/PLIF feedback. Next connect the rail transfer to realistic signed float fan-in and test near-cancellation before any acceptance decision.

2026-09-30: Added full-float analog dynamic-range/noise screening across25 PLIF actors. ## VCM-referenced MOS hold replay and retention gate — 2026-10-01

Completed two consecutive full-frame, all-neuron epoch58 float replays using the corrected extracted-bank VCM differential hold profile. All278,784,000 state accesses per frame fell inside the measured voltage range. Deterministic retention perturbation produced1.55% final-fuse spike mismatch and91.7–91.9% pixel agreement on the two frames. Adding an independent9.5µV RMS reset-noise proxy across three seeds per frame produced1.78–1.92% spike mismatch and89.9–90.7% pixel agreement. These results are limited to two frames and an idealized software noise injection; they are not device noise or yield measurements. The extracted-bank five-ms hold itself still drifts up to6.228mV at the tested rails. Passive MOS gate storage is not a viable retained membrane cell at this operating point. Continue with shared active differential charge-bucket plus an independent analog state store; characterize restore/commit service and actual full-float error before layout.

## Active switched-capacitor bucket with full-float fan-in — 2026-10-02

The physical SKY130 OTA/MIM active bucket now replays actual epoch58 full-float event multisets on near-threshold and max-fanin sites. Ideal packet voltages and a shared0.78 analog gain factor put the near-threshold shuffle within1.02µV and the max-fanin shuffle within36.8µV of target; max-fanin reverse order remains0.310mV off. The simulated schedules fit inside5ms, but exclude graph/reset/readout overhead. The OTA common mode drifts≈30–36mV by5ms, hidden by matched-reference subtraction. This is an active-bucket screen, not a qualified neuron: the weight-to-charge driver remains ideal, with noise/PVT/mismatch, threshold/reset/beta, and extracted layout untested. Next build a physical differential/reference version and quantify common-mode drift, then reassess macro layout.


### Active bucket corner and reference-offset extension — 2026-10-02

The near-threshold active-bucket replay was repeated at SKY130 SS/0°C and FF/85°C with the TT-calibrated shared packet gain0.78. The expected full-float incremental state is+32.623µV; the measured signal-minus-no-event-reference changes are+31.2µV at SS/0°C, +31.6µV at TT/27°C, and+29.2µV at FF/85°C. Their errors are−1.42,−1.02, and−3.42µV, respectively. The event contribution stays correct-sign, but the FF error approaches the selected site's≈4.09µV float threshold margin. The absolute no-event OTA output is0.894V at SS/0°C and0.413V at FF/85°C at5ms (versus0.9V VCM); the differential readout only appears stable because a matched reference branch is subtracted.

A deterministic+0.1mV perturbation to the neutral reference OTA's bias source moves the near-threshold event-minus-neutral result to+0.676mV, versus+31.7µV with zero offset. This is not a statistical mismatch model, but it shows that the present reference subtraction cannot be trusted without a physical correlated reference/offset calibration path. Continue with a differential active bucket and physical common-mode/offset control; the current single-ended OTA bucket is not ready for layout release or full-float acceptance. Reports are listed under `physical_active_bucket_pvt_screen` and `physical_active_bucket_reference_offset_stress` in the architecture boundary JSON.

### Complementary active bucket phase correction and float replay — 2026-10-02

The first differential-pair failure was a runner timing bug: the packet-connect switch overlapped packet-reset release. The passing single-bucket deck uses a dead interval, then a later connect window. Matching that exact phase sequence restores transfer. One positive event measures0.785µV half-difference against0.801µV target. Full near-threshold epoch58 float fan-in (329 events, gain0.78) errors are+1.135µV forward,+0.137µV reverse,+0.515µV shuffle at TT; SS/0°C shuffle is+0.259µV and FF/85°C shuffle−0.675µV. The same near-threshold shuffle finishes at common mode0.894V SS,0.868V TT,0.413V FF. High-cancellation shuffle error is+0.451µV for a7.5nV target; max-fanin shuffle error is+0.247mV for−52.454mV target. These remain ideal packet-driver schematic results, without threshold/readout, mismatch/noise, extraction, or layout.

A first transistor CMFB driving the OTA load-bias node held FF common mode near0.9005V but collapsed differential state to1.56µV; reversing its input sign drove the outputs to ground. Moving its control to weak output PFET assist left FF common mode at0.417V and made differential error+0.212mV. Reject these CMFB versions. The paired differential bucket is a viable schematic charge-transfer lead, not a qualified neuron. Next quantify full-float threshold decisions using the paired output, determine whether common-mode range is compatible with a physical comparator at FF, then implement a physical event-weight driver and extracted OTA/MIM macro after its noise/mismatch gates. Reports and runner are named in `physical_active_bucket_complementary_pair_phase_corrected` in the architecture boundary JSON and `scripts/test_sky130_sc_integrator_differential_pair.py`.

### Differential-bucket threshold latch across corners — 2026-10-02

Connected the complementary active-bucket output to the existing SKY130 regenerative latch, using an ideal differential threshold source set from the original epoch58 float threshold. With a5ns transient maximum, the TT near-threshold case resolves to the correct no-spike output; the measured bucket event increment is32.073µV against32.623µV target. At FF/85°C, the same untrimmed latch resolves to the opposite polarity (false spike) even though the bucket event increment is32.036µV, within0.59µV of target. Applying ideal threshold shifts of+2.5µV,+3.75µV and+5µV brackets the FF latch crossing:3.75µV still misclassifies;5µV classifies this selected case correctly. This only establishes a corner-specific offset target; it is not a physical trim, mismatch distribution, noise, or full-margin decision pass.

A 20µm physical bottom-plate MIM/TG level-shift sampler raises the FF latch input common mode to about0.900V, but its off-state loading shifts the bucket event result by roughly0.590mV over the5ms bin. Its sampled input is about−208µV, far from the expected few-microvolt decision residual. Reject this sampler as implemented. Next build a low-common-mode-compatible regenerative input stage or a buffered/trimmed readout, characterize its offset over float-margin distribution and PVT, then return to extracted macro layout. The active-bucket charge core still has no physical weight encoder or qualified initial-state loader; do not claim PLIF acceptance or full-chip layout. Evidence is in `physical_active_bucket_threshold_latch_screen` in the boundary JSON and the referenced result reports.


### Complementary latch and corner-dependent offset — 2026-10-02

Added an experimental clocked PMOS input pair to the regenerative threshold latch runner. At minimum legal width (0.42µm per input PFET, 1.68µm tail), the actual 329-event epoch58 near-threshold trace is classified correctly at TT and FF using the float-derived ideal threshold. The paired bucket increment errors remain−0.460µV (TT) and−0.560µV (FF); latch read disturbance is0.028µV and0.030µV. At SS/0°C this sizing produces a false spike, and the unassisted latch also false-spikes. An ideal threshold shift of−5µV changes the SS sampled differential to+2.565µV and restores the no-spike result; +5µV does not. The FF prior bracket needed a positive shift, so trim must be corner calibrated. A 0.1× PFET sizing attempt failed model resolution because W=0.2µm is below the supported model minimum; it was invalid, not a failed circuit simulation.

This remains one-site schematic evidence with ideal threshold reference/packets and no mismatch, switched noise, physical trim, extracted layout, threshold variation, or full-frame replay. The fixed complementary input pair and unassisted latch are both unqualified. Next implement a physical differential auto-zero/trim path and evaluate the observed float-margin distribution, then return to layout once state storage and system service gates also pass. JSON: `physical_active_bucket_complementary_input_latch_screen`.


### Full-float comparator margin budget — 2026-10-02

Analyzed all1,966,080 final-fusion candidate values in the verified epoch58 float trace. Under the current exploratory SKY130 mapping, absolute margin quantiles are0.537µV at the0.1 percentile,5.28µV at1%, and54.5µV at5%;0.20% of all trace values lie within1µV of threshold and0.95% within5µV. Among float spikes, the0.1-percentile margin is0.264µV. These are mapped sensitivity values, not an accepted physical scaling, and the trace covers sampled patches rather than full-dataset process validation. They show the selected ±5µV trim experiment is far too narrow to certify readout accuracy: physical comparator offset/noise must be characterized against the complete margin histogram, followed by full-float replay with measured error. The diagnostic script is `scripts/analyze_sky130_float_threshold_margin_distribution.py`; JSON is `sky130_float_threshold_margin_distribution.json`.


A zero-mean independent Gaussian perturbation applied analytically to these saved margins predicts0.077% state-decision flips at1µV RMS (0.146% of float spikes) and0.375% at5µV RMS (0.693% of float spikes). This is a sensitivity calculation only; it excludes temporal/systematic correlations, neuron feedback, downstream propagation and task accuracy. Use it to set circuit noise/offset characterization points, not as an analog simulation result.


### Full-frame comparator-noise and nonlinear-scale replay — 2026-10-02

Extended the epoch58 full-float replay with independent voltage-equivalent noise at every PLIF threshold decision. This is a software sensitivity test, not measured PDK noise. With a per-actor linear ±0.9V mapping and0.1µV RMS threshold noise across all actors, one complete 440×640 validation frame has1.68–1.93% final-fuse spike mismatch and89.4–90.6% pixel agreement across three seeds; labeled accuracy changes from49.82% float to49.50–49.99%. At0.25µV RMS, pixel agreement is86.2–86.9% and accuracy49.16–49.73%.

An ideal asinh mapping at range gain1000 improves one-frame pixel agreement to97.14–98.61% under0.1µV noise. An ideal 8-segment piecewise approximation at the same gain gives99.972–99.994% agreement on record0 and99.21–99.99% on record1; full-float labeled accuracy stays within about0.05 percentage points of each record's baseline across tested seeds. This suggests that nonlinear state scaling can make comparator noise less damaging without quantizing the model. It does not validate the encoding hardware: the piecewise map, range sensing/switching, charge updates and retention are ideal, and the noise is synthetic. Continue by implementing a physical range-coded analog state update and repeat the full-frame replay with measured transient/mismatch errors before layout. JSON evidence is `sky130_full_float_threshold_noise_replay_boundary.json` and `full_float_threshold_noise_sensitivity_replay` in the architecture boundary.

# 2026-10-02: Shared OTA CMFB polarity resolved; controller still unqualified

Measured the shared output PMOS gate's DC control transfer and the CMFB OTA input polarity in TT/27°C, SS/0°C and FF/85°C. `mean -> OTA inn` is the correcting sign: as mean exceeds 0.9V, controller output goes high and turns down the PMOS loads. The reversed sign drives the gate low and rails the state outputs high. The correctly signed loop still moves/oscillates over the 5ms transient; its steep DC switching around the 0.9V crossing indicates the present OTA controller has excessive effective gain or unsuitable bias for this servo. Added reproducible `scripts/screen_sky130_cmfb_ota_polarity.py`. Next reduce controller gain/bandwidth and establish a stable zero-event operating point across corners. No layout or event/full-float qualification is claimed.

A passive gate feedback variant was stable but inaccurate across corners: with a0.9V bias reference, settled common mode was0.113V TT,0.029V SS and1.011V FF. At a TT trim of0.812V, a1mV differential state fell below0.105mV by100µs and to zero at1ms; thus the feedback path erases the represented differential state. Reject passive feedback and simple trim. Low-gain OTA variants also wander/rail. Continue with a controller that senses common mode without loading the differential state or a different active differential architecture; layout stays gated.

Added source degeneration to the CMFB OTA input legs and swept 100Ω–10kΩ plus compensation and tail bias. At100Ω/100pF/0.45V tail bias, TT common mode is0.927V at5ms, but SS rails low to0.0003V and FF settles0.662V. No set passes PVT. Source degeneration alone is not sufficient; proceed to a structurally bounded common-mode controller or alternate differential OTA topology.

### Shared-reset timing and reset-noise replay — 2026-10-02

Opening the inter-rail bridge while both VCM clamps remain on reduces the deterministic differential kick in the extracted TG/pair transient from 1.243µV to 0.132nV. The rails stay clamped for another 10µs before release. This is a sequencing result only: the deterministic simulator has no thermal noise or mismatch, and the independent-reset comparator also remains near zero in that model. It does not establish correlated reset noise. Reports and deck are `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_ss0` and `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_clamp_last_ss0`.

Replayed one full DSEC frame from the verified epoch58 full-float checkpoint with measured TT 5ms pair retention, ideal piecewise8 state encoding, and illustrative independent 24µV RMS differential reset noise. Three seeds with noise only at frame initialization gave 99.931–99.939% pixel agreement at range gain100, 99.918–99.919% at1000, and99.864% at10000. Reapplying the same noise after every subtractive spike reset gave99.927–99.935%,99.918–99.919%, and99.863–99.865%, respectively. Final-fusion spike mismatch remained below0.10%; labeled pixel accuracy remained near the float baseline49.82%. These results show the previous conclusion that 24µV reset noise necessarily destroys frame fidelity was tied to a less suitable state map/gain case. This replay remains a sensitivity test: piecewise encoding/update is ideal, the Gaussian is not a PDK transient-noise waveform, and the single frame does not qualify the circuit.

Next characterize the physical noise and state-dependent error of the extracted event injector against the original-float high-cancellation, maximum-fanin and near-threshold sequences. Build/extract a reset-and-state macro only after the injector and readout topology are credible; then run PVT/noise and replay measured errors. No full PLIF or chip acceptance is claimed.

2026-10-03 continuation: added coarse/fine differential MIM charge-injection trim to the buffered readout bench. The coarse bank alone had no shared code for the float-derived ±0.537257883 µV q0.1 pair. An 8-bit coarse plus 8-bit fine code passes both signs at 5 ms in TT for one deterministic ±1σ latch mismatch pair (33/255) and its mirror (222/0). These endpoint codes use ideal plate controls. A paired extracted MIM/TG primitive transient passes coarse and fine step checks across TT/SS/FF; no complete weighted array, array PEX, mismatch/noise, calibration-search sequencer, PVT latch-offset sweep, or original-float replay exists. Next route/extract a differential weighted slice, measure transfer and switch feedthrough across PVT, then run the 5 ms q0.1 and full-float decision screens with the extracted transfer. Keep readout/neuron layout gated.

2026-10-04 physical trim array: added the routed 4-bank, 8-bit coarse/fine SKY130 MIM array (1020 minimum cells, 767.62×197.755µm), with Magic DRC=0, unique Netgen LVS, and extracted interconnect capacitance. Its 96 plate-select TGs/decoder are not in the GDS; they remain schematic PEX instances. Integrated the array into the readout bench and added adjustable coarse plate swing.

The first physical-array readout point reused a prior ideal code and drove about−30mV onto the latch input. Re-centering to coarse128 and scaling the coarse swing to2mV reduced the differential correction to the microvolt range while preserving chopper sign and sample-state disturbance at the report resolution. However, under the published deterministic ±1σ latch input-pair model, the negative q0.1 decision still fails. A 10mV coarse range sweeps the extracted latch input differential beyond±4.7mV and still fails to reverse that result. The same negative input passes without the mismatch model. The separate SKY130 sensor-pair probe reports a2.13mV input-referred crossing; no common calibrated code, yield result, PVT mismatch sweep, or full-float decision pass is established. This rejects the current direct regenerative-latch path as qualified. Next test a preamplified/auto-zeroed comparator with explicit input-referred offset and measured pre-evaluation gates; only then finish selector/decoder layout and the5ms/full-float screens. Do not promote the current trim macro to a qualified readout.

2026-10-04 correction to the preliminary latch-offset conclusion above: the earlier coarse-midpoint tests did not reject the latch after a code search; a 20mV coarse plate span and reduced 0.1mV fine span found one common code. At TT/27°C, coarse37/fine80 passes both ±0.537257883µV test signs at the5ms endpoint under the same deterministic +1σ/−1σ latch input pair. Each run includes the extracted 1020-cell MIM array and 96 extracted TG PEX instances, but not selector/decoder layout. Chopper input sign is correct in TT; input-equivalent errors are−0.321µV and−0.265µV, so only sign classification is demonstrated, not analog-value accuracy. The state read disturbance is zero at report resolution.

The same fixed trim fails at SS/0°C and FF/85°C: positive input chopper sign reverses and latch decisions fail, despite preserved MIM state. SS remains wrong with a10ns transient step and with VCM=1.05V or the high-resistor corner. Thus the TT common code is not PVT robust and the buffered chopper front end must be corrected before claiming readout completion. Previous DRC/LVS/P EX figures remain valid for the capacitor array; selector/decoder GDS is still open and now gated on a usable PVT-stable readout architecture.
2026-10-04 readout continuation: the SS buffered-chopper polarity error persisted when sampling aperture was reduced from2µs to0.1µs (SS/0°C, 30µs diagnostic bin; input sign and latch classification still wrong). This closes the local timing-aperture hypothesis. The routed two-cell differential trim MIM pair was then simulated with its extracted coupling caps, extracted state/sample MIM and PDK TGs at SS/TT/FF/27°C. Its transfer is consistent to <0.06% across those corners, reaching≈456µV correction at rail-safe ±0.5V plate excursions. The pair is too small by itself to correct the multi-millivolt offsets in the large-state direct latch. Continue by evaluating a larger extracted trim bank or smaller comparator input capacitance; selector/decoder, latch integration, noise/mismatch, temperature and float-trace gates remain open.
2026-10-04 weighted-bank direct-latch integration: the routed 1,020-cell coarse/fine MIM array PEX was connected directly to the membrane-sampling nodes in the direct late-bin comparator. The bounded TT, one-sign, 30µs transient completed and resolved the sign, but charge sharing and trim plate switching changed stored differential state by1.362mV (537.288µV before read;1,899.243µV afterward). Reject this direct connection. The next valid integration must buffer/isolate the sampled membrane before connecting trim plates; state disturbance is a hard gate before PVT/5ms work.
2026-10-04 buffered readout trim continuation: the array already has a separate OTA-buffered comparator node, so the SS trim search was repeated there rather than on the disturbing direct connection. With ideal plate-selection waveforms and the routed array PEX, SS/0°C code125/231 resolves both mapped±0.537257883µV signs at the5ms endpoint; FF/85°C code125/220 also resolves both at5ms. Membrane disturbance is0 at the report's resolution. Calibration is corner-dependent and does not repair the analog chopper value: positive inputs still produce wrong-sign chopped output (about−25.5µV SS and−71.5µV FF versus+5.43µV expected in the tested mapping). This is a threshold-only deterministic screen; mismatch/noise, selector/decoder layout, value accuracy and original-float replay remain open.
2026-10-04 SS intermediate-temperature follow-up: the 0°C trim did not transfer to SS/27°C. A short search found code126/55; both mapped±0.537µV cases then passed the5ms latch at SS/27°C, with no report-resolution state disturbance. The output polarity is correct, but the chopped values are+0.662/−9.135µV against expected±5.426µV, showing substantial offset/gain error. Calibration must accommodate temperature; comparator sign screening does not qualify analog-value fidelity.
2026-10-04 zero-crossing diagnostic: at SS/27°C, code126/55 and exactly0µV differential cause transient timestep underflow at19.8431µs on the regenerative latch clock branch. Treat exact equality as metastable, not as a simulator pass. Any analog auto-calibration must bracket with finite signed dither/hysteresis, then prove the deadband stays below the original-float threshold-margin distribution.
2026-10-04 threshold-resolution follow-up: SS/27°C code126/53 classifies both±0.25µV and±0.537257883µV mapped cases in short-bin PEX-array runs, and both q0.1 signs at5ms. However q0.1 chopper outputs remain+0.6605/−9.1333µV versus expected±5.4263µV. Exposed the physical feedback-resistor ratio as a `--gain` characterization parameter and screened9.0/10.1/11.2; raw output remains≈0.62–0.66µV while expected shifts4.84–6.02µV, with input-referred error≈−0.47µV. Reject simple gain-ratio adjustment; pursue the dominant analog offset/transfer error.

2026-10-04 continuation: SS/27°C code126/54 subsequently passes the finite 5ms latch screen at both signs of ±0.25µV and ±0.537257883µV, with membrane disturbance below report resolution. This supersedes code126/53's +0.25µV long-bin failure. It does not cure sampled analog-value error: output remains offset/asymmetric (about +0.66/−9.13µV for expected ±5.43µV at the outer pair). Exactly zero input remains metastable, so calibration must use finite dither/bracketing. The full-float sensitivity report was recalculated with affine raw transfer and inferred trimmed latch threshold; its ≈5.09e−7 wrong-decision fraction is only a two-point extrapolation over saved final-fusion margins, not circuit replay or acceptance. The routed 1020-cell MIM array remains a primitive: 96 selector TGs and decoder are schematic in the bench and need physical implementation plus extracted simulation. Readout value accuracy, mismatch/noise, full-float event replay, retention/service and full-chip assembly remain open.

2026-10-04 physical-selector progress: a reusable three-TG plate-selector cell now has generated GDS, Magic DRC0, unique Netgen LVS, and extracted PEX with64 coupling capacitors. At TT/27°C, SS/0°C, and FF/85°C, extracted simulation precharges a10fF plate to0.900000V, drives HI=0.980000V through nearly5ms, and switches to LO=0.820000V. This is a switch primitive result with ideal controls; it does not close the 96-TG selector/decoder or readout gates. Next tile32 selector bits and route the full plate interface to the weighted MIM array, then simulate decoded trim codes against the buffered/latch path.

2026-10-04 continued layout/sim work: completed a physically placed32-bit (96-TG) trim selector bank and routed its32 plate outputs into the1020-unit weighted MIM array. Both bank and combined top pass Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS; extracted bank simulation drives all10fF plate loads at TT through5ms. Full combined PDK-MIM simulation fails: after reset and code126/129/54/201, membrane-equivalent state drifts from≈0.899V to≈0.910V at4.9ms. Diagnostic isolates the shift to the large SKY130 MIM device model: it appears with the standard/reference1020-cap netlist and with ideal state clamps (≈157pA per state at equal0.9V terminals), but not with ideal9.52fF capacitors. Next isolate the PDK model's large-population DC/transient behavior and validate initialization/model parameter usage. Do not advance this bank to comparator/readout acceptance until full5ms state stability passes. This new result supersedes the earlier intended next step of declaring bank integration ready for trim-code simulation.

2026-10-04 MIM model-population follow-up: full isolated SKY130 model array has≈157pA/state clamp current at equal 0.9V terminals, but one/16 devices do not; a prefix sweep reaches≈18–20pA at 256–512 and rises to≈157pA while adding instances 513–768. `dev/gauss` removal and tighter/smaller `gmin` did not repair it. An ideal-zero `rs2` diagnostic removes isolated current but makes the integrated transient stiff enough to hit the 180s timeout near0.09ms, so it is rejected as a solution. Next locate the model/population interaction across bank grouping and parameter sets, then restore a standard-model integrated 5ms run before proceeding with membrane/readout coupling. Do not accept the currently DRC/LVS-clean top for functional layout.

2026-10-04 MIM isolation detail: independently isolated coarse-P, fine-P, and fine-N banks (255 cells each) have similar≈18pA ideal-clamp current; the combined populated network grows substantially. Sharing one plate bus leaves the effect; sharing both state rails reduces it but still gives≈122pA. Current points to cross-population/model-numerics interaction, not a uniquely defective bank. Continue paired-bank/subckt scaling and compare with an independent simulator or a minimal model reproducer, keeping all production runs on the unmodified PDK.

2026-10-04 simulator cross-check: built ngspice47 (official source SHA-256 checked, KLU enabled) as a separate image. The isolated full-array equal-voltage test matches ngspice36 at≈156.7pA per rail, ruling out the age of the existing simulator build. The integrated 5ms macro test is much slower in ngspice47 and timed out at180s around0.78ms; that partial result is rejected, and the already-completed ngspice36 integrated report remains the standard-model gate. Diagnose the PDK model’s apparent DC path next; do not accept a modified model.

Current path is present already at DC operating point in both ngspice36 and ngspice47 (≈156.7pA/state, equal 0.9V terminals). A 1e15Ω MIM-terminal bleed does not alter it; changing only the diagnostic via-resistance model parameter swings the clamp current by orders of magnitude with no external voltage change. Treat this as unresolved numerical/model operating-point sensitivity, not established physical leakage, and retain the production PDK unchanged. Next inspect matrix/internal device currents or produce a minimal reproducer and correct initialization before accepting the integrated state bank.

2026-10-04 update: isolated standard and native C-R-R MIM benches reproduce ≈14.5mV common-mode drift; explicit initial conditions on every model-internal node do not cure it. For5ms service transients, replaced only the nominal PDK MIM cell’s≈0.1Ω/9.52fF (≈0.93fs) internal series network with a direct9.52fF capacitor, retaining extracted GDS interconnect parasitics and all selector devices. The integrated routed32-bit top then completes TT/27°C through4.9ms with state movement≤0.174µV after update and72.042µV trim differential. This supports the time-scale reduction for module transient analysis; the unmodified cell model’s sub-fs startup remains a simulator/model limitation to be kept separately visible, not an accepted PDK transient pass. Next exercise switching/5ms retention across state codes and PVT, then connect analog readout and full-float event replay.

2026-10-04 continuation: after adopting the reduced-order long-bin MIM model, the actual routed weighted array/selector bank was integrated with the extracted MIM readout sample pair and schematic chopper/latch. SS/27°C with calibration code126/fine54 resolves both signs at±0.537257883µV and±0.25µV in a5ms bin, with zero state kick reported. The analog value transfer remains inaccurate and asymmetric (for example +0.537µV→+0.661µV and−0.537µV→−9.134µV against symmetric±5.426µV targets); at+0.25µV its raw value sign is wrong while the calibrated latch sign remains right. Continue by fixing/calibrating value readout, then search PVT-safe threshold codes and run mismatch/noise plus original-float replay. This is one deterministic SS corner, ideal decode controls, no Monte Carlo/noise and reduced ESR; no full model or full-chip acceptance.


2026-10-04 physical trim sampling repair (benchv3): corrected benchv2 signed500us TT pair at code127/143 measured +34.635883/+33.407730uV pre-clock latch inputs for +/-0.25uV, although the held sample difference changed by8.948129uV. Time-series inspection located the loss at plate selection: precharge had been released before the buffer sampled onto the array, leaving plate nodes floating. Keep plate precharge until after buffer isolation and release it before HI/LO selection (break-before-make). Benchv3 then measures +38.631226/+29.680419uV, a separation8.950806uV versus sampled8.948622uV, preserving the signal through the physical array. Negative decision still fails because the approximately34uV offset is uncorrected; code127/143 is rejected as calibrated. Continue actual pre-clock calibration with code127/56 at5mV coarse and0.1mV fine plate spans. These are reduced-MIM, deterministic TT500us diagnostics with ideal controls, not noise/mismatch/full5ms or chip acceptance.


2026-10-04 corrected full5ms signed readout: benchv3 TT/27C, reduced MIM explicitly included, physical weighted-array/individual-selector PEX, code127/48, coarse5mV/fine0.1mV,100us phase dwell. Both +/-0.25uV runs complete. Pre-clock latch inputs +4.350615/-4.598827uV with clock0V; both decisions correct. Held phase differences +4.623312/-4.325023uV, chopped values +2.427845/-2.268467uV. Simulated differential state disturbance magnitude below2.4e-7uV (deterministic solver output, not a physical noise floor). The old claim that long amplifier settling alone caused the error is not established: model inclusion, sample/chopper overlap and plate-clamp sequence were also defective. A10us dwell signed pair is being evaluated to separate these effects and reduce service cost. This result does not qualify PVT, noise/mismatch, a physical clock/reference generator, original-float accuracy, population service or full-chip layout.


2026-10-04 signed5ms sequence result: benchv3 code127/48 at5mV/0.1mV plate swings passes both +/-0.25uV decisions at100us and10us dwell in TT/27C. At10us dwell the pre-clock inputs are +4.347047/-4.589742uV. The30us diagnostic readout window is not proof of5ms population service. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_readout_sequence_repair_summary.json. Additional algebra correction: swapping both inputs gives ideal demodulated gain G-0.5, so nominal G=10.1 implies9.6, not10.1. Benchv4 corrects future expected/error fields; source benchv3 raw data is unchanged and the summary supplies corrected errors. Next qualify corrected sequencing across PVT, mismatch/noise and model-derived margins, then characterize actual service/area and finish reusable readout layout. Full-float and full-chip acceptance remain open.


2026-10-04 settled readout PVT and replication gate: benchv5 holds the trim plates clamped during sampling and delays latch evaluation3.4us after plate selection. The earlier SS code64 pass depended on the selector transient and is superseded. At5ms with10us phase dwell, deterministic +/-0.25uV decisions pass at TT/27C (fine48), SS/0C (fine72), FF/85C (fine48), common coarse127 and5mV/0.1mV plate spans. Max measured input-referred chopped error across these six cases is0.0360uV. The SS code was calibrated from the actual settled latch decisions; no on-chip calibration controller or mismatch/noise yield is shown. Source-hashed result: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_readout_settled_pvt_population_gate.json. Area/service bound: a sequential30us readout for30,976,000 distinct resident states once per5ms needs at least185,856 parallel channels with zero overhead. The routed selector/trim GDS bounding box is1173.675x199.245um, so direct replication alone would occupy43,462mm2 in bounding boxes, excluding state storage and readout amplifiers. This disqualifies direct replication of this large trim macro as the full-chip architecture. Keep it as a physical readout demonstrator; next pursue a much smaller auto-zero/calibrated decision cell or an alternative state/readout organization, with original-float accuracy, noise/mismatch and actual service accounting still required.
2026-10-04 analog continuation: established six deterministic5ms signed readout decisions across TT/SS/FF with settled benchv5, then screened direct replication. Extracted sample-bank area and existing OTA spectrum show the large sampled readout is a module demonstrator only: 4,751mm2 two-bank floor at minimum zero-overhead channel count, conditional1.014uV input-equivalent sampling kT/C versus exploratory0.537uV q0.1 full-float margin, and123uV small-signal differential unity-buffer output noise before gain. Continued fixed-cap live-range event bench with branch-current probes: TT/SS/FF positive crossings are measurable, but FF pulse gate1.0→1.1V changes event update84.677→21.436mV and shifts selector timing. See `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_readout_sample_noise_service_gate.json` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_piecewise_branch_charge_summary.json`. Analog full-float module, compact layout and full-chip assembly remain open.
2026-10-04 original-float replay continuation: two complete validation frames with ideal six-range encoding, measured SS retention and24.80086uV independent threshold-noise proxy from the SS 100-device direct-sensor spectrum give mean present-class IoU0.20402/0.20080 versus float0.20573/0.19892. Pixel agreement is86.35%/85.25%; final-fusion spike mismatch2.61%/2.77%. This is a two-frame ideal sensitivity bound and does not qualify the physical encoder or sensor. Source-hashed summary: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_companded_sensor_noise_replay_summary.json`.
2026-10-04 state-cell correction: full-float ideal six-range replay was repeated with kT/C proxy scaled to the actual20x20um fixed-MIM crossing cell and legal minimum2x2um MIM (no hold). Both retain roughly baseline two-frame mean IoU under the optimistic ideal encoding, while the20um bare plate inventory floor is12,390mm2. The routed minimum2x2um MIM/TG 5ms SS physical hold fails by+166/−149mV with substrate-grounded diagnostic PEX; two stacked routed TGs do not improve it. The original PEX `VSUBS` was floating, so uncorrected ideal-control drift is invalid. Full analog state cell and full-chip remain unqualified.
2026-10-04 off-switch isolation: corrected an invalid `.ic` startup and ran ideal programming into the routed minimum MIM at SS/0C. The bare state holds, the off NFET/PFET pull in opposite directions by hundreds of millivolts, and their long-channel variants do not materially reduce5ms drift. The standard-MIM cross-check agrees in direction/magnitude. Current public ICsprout55/OpenRPDK28 collateral does not justify a ready denser analog PDK migration. Continue analog state architecture research; see `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary_summary.json` and `docs/RESULTS.md`.
2026-10-05 signed fixed-cap injector continuation: physical NFET sink plus reversed comparator gave individually tuned negative live-boundary crossings at TT/27C, SS/0C and FF/85C, with one positive TT control, all 1.5us. A direct two-event fixed-MIM screen at one gate pair nearly balances TT (+9.055/−9.120mV; `pn`−79.1uV), but fixed gates fail across corners: SS `pn`−0.874mV and FF `pn`+49.005mV. The TT `pn`/`np` order spread is16.7uV; FF spread1.657mV. These are deterministic ideal-gate schematic diagnostics, without live selector in the sequence, physical weight/bias control, 5ms state hold or full-float network. Source-hashed reports: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_piecewise_signed_crossing_summary.json` and `sky130_piecewise_signed_sequence_corner_summary.json`. Continue physical calibrated signed delivery and compact low-leak state research; layout remains gated.
2026-10-05 manual gate calibration follow-up: independently fitted ideal SS and FF gate pairs recover roughly balanced ±10mV single events at0.9V, but `pn`/`np` residuals remain−35.2/−14.7uV SS and−30.6/−11.5uV FF. Across TT/SS/FF the order spread is about17–21uV. This local manual fit does not supply a physical state-aware weight driver or meet original-float margin, and cannot resolve the separate5ms state-store failure. Same source-hashed sequence summary includes the fitted cases.
2026-10-05 compressed-state retention check: the routed minimum MIM/TG at SS/0C loses about52% of a ±1mV or ±10mV offset from0.9V in5ms; bare MIM controls hold. This rules out simply keeping this accessed cell near VCM to preserve microvolt information. The wide-range fixed-current area extrapolation in the same summary is conditional and inapplicable to compressed states because their leakage current scales down with offset. Alternative analog isolation/storage or a measured event-time-aware state correction remains necessary. See `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary_summary.json`.
2026-10-05 event-time diagnostic: identical ideal11pA×1us pulses at0.5ms and4.5ms on the routed off-TG minimum MIM leave0.4174mV and0.7499mV at5ms after matched no-event subtraction (early/late ratio0.557). The100ns and50ns maximum-step runs agree and their immediate increments are0.8075/0.8068mV. The original1us-step late-pulse result was a numerical time-step artifact and is superseded. This accessed state cell is event-time dependent and does not implement the full-float bin accumulation semantics. Evidence is source-hashed in the same hold boundary summary.
2026-10-05 learned-beta comparison: verified original float parameter export has1,856 channel beta values, median0.938 and1,804 at least0.9; only6 are below0.6. The accessed minimum MIM/TG loses about52% of a small state offset in5ms, so its physical continuous leak is not a direct substitute for the learned boundary beta. The source hash and unweighted distribution are in the hold boundary summary.
2026-10-05 near-VCM isolation follow-up: two routed off TGs in series give essentially the same5ms SS/0C drift as one at±1mV and±10mV; e.g. low-state+0.5219mV versus+0.5216mV at1mV. The simple stack is rejected as a retention repair in this grounded-substrate diagnostic PEX model. Continue searching for a different analog isolation/storage topology.
## Body-tied SKY130 switch hold boundary — 2026-10-05

Built an isolated-well TG probe with DNW tap and separate NFET/PFET body ports; its6.25×16.345µm GDS has Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS. The first low-drift hold test parked each off-switch source at its state's starting voltage, an optimistic control. Adding both PDK well-diode geometry proxies to that control gives TT/27°C ±1mV differential drift−14.0µV over5ms. A corrected shared0.9V source bus gives−0.7315mV differential drift at ±1mV and−353.257mV at ±300mV, even with DNW biased to1.2V for near-zero common-mode drift. Equal early/late ideal events retain only0.315/0.453mV at the bin end despite equal immediate0.474mV increments; 25ns step refinement confirms the difference. This topology fails the active5ms membrane and within-bin event-time gates. The diode geometry is inferred from drawn regions and body connections are not physically routed, so no foundry signoff is claimed. Continue full-float analog work on storage isolation and physical read/restore service. Details: `docs/RESULTS.md`.

A passive second extracted TG plus ideal floating guard cap improves the early/late final ratio to0.773 at10fF, while100fF gives0.718; both retain substantial event-time error. The unshared four-TG footprint scales to12,658mm² for the resident population before guard/state capacitors. This passive guard is not selected. Current official public ICsprout55/OpenRPDK28 sources still lack a ready smaller-node analog model/extraction stack, so the next effort remains physical analog state read/restore rather than a node-name migration.

2026-10-05 shared-bus correction screen: reran the conventional extracted small MIM/TG with fixed0.9V off-switch bus at TT/27, SS/0 and FF/85, at both ±1mV and ±300mV. The 5ms differential retention range is0.4744–0.4804, implying ideal inverse read-error gain2.081–2.108; FF near VCM shifts both rails upward by about43mV. This is deterministic conditioning only, not an accepted read/restore circuit. Existing large sampled readout fails direct population area/service scaling. Continue a compact differential sensor and physically scheduled state writeback, with noise/mismatch and original-float semantics still gating layout. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_fixed_bus_hold_correction_boundary.json`.

2026-10-05 passive sensor-gate follow-up: schematic10×1µm NMOS inputs attached to the extracted small MIM/TG retain89.5–89.6% of a ±1mV differential state over5ms with the sensor fully unpowered across TT/SS/FF, versus47.4–48.0% without the gates. An always-on sensor at TT draws27.83µA, about1,552W if unshared across all states. Powering a previously unpowered direct sensor at4.99ms causes≈522mV common-mode state jump at TT even with a1µs ramp, so direct power gating is rejected. Treat passive gate capacitance as the next analog physical candidate and isolate the read connection. Source-hashed evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_passive_gate_sensor_boundary.json`.

2026-10-05 shared-read check: a TT prebiased shared differential sensor connected through extracted TGs to the passive physical MOSCAP/MIM state gives a9.93mV output difference, but the stored differential drops1.6155→0.8206mV during the read window and common mode moves+4.21mV. The simple isolated connection is destructive at this scale; redesign the read coupling before combined-cell layout. Same source-hashed passive-gate boundary report.

2026-10-05 minimum-input read screen: reducing the shared sensor inputs from10×1µm to0.42×1µm improves TT read-window differential retention from50.8% to92.5%, but leaves+4.76mV common-mode kick and0.2968mV differential loss against the no-read state at5ms. The3.483mV output is measurable in this deterministic simulation; offset/noise and full-float accuracy are untested. Continue isolated/charge-balanced read design; source-hashed in the passive-gate boundary report.

2026-10-05 read-switch decomposition: with0.42µm sensor inputs, replacing only the extracted read TG by an ideal switch yields1.7385mV end differential versus1.7916mV no-read; the physical switch gives1.4948mV. Its off-state already lowers the preconnect span1.7920→1.6155mV, and activation adds the rest plus+4.76mV common-mode kick. Focus physical read isolation and charge balancing; ideal sharing still causes≈53µV loss. Source-hashed in the passive-gate boundary report.

2026-10-05 boundary-read reinterpretation and signed screen: destructive read can be valid if each state is calibrated and immediately rewritten at its mandatory bin update. The existing TG plus0.42µm shared sensor produces TT −3.4813/0/+3.4828mV output for commanded −1/0/+1mV rail offsets and positive sign at SS/FF; a schematic minimum TG only modestly improves state disturbance. Stationary small-input sensor output noise is0.5733mV RMS over10Hz–100kHz, corresponding to an illustrative164.65µV rail-equivalent under the local TT transfer. An optimistic two-complete-frame original-float replay with ideal six-range encoding and165µV independent threshold noise yields mean IoU0.2001–0.2028/0.1981–0.2013 versus float0.2057/0.1989. This omits physical read/write error, mismatch, retention, calibration and service. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_destructive_read_boundary.json`.

2026-10-05 full-range read screen: completed TT offsets +450,+750,+900mV. Direct sensor output nearly saturates by+750mV: 750→900mV gain is only0.008246mV/mV, about422× below its near-zero gain. The current single-range shared sensor cannot supply uniform full-range state precision. Proceed with a physical range-selected/level-shifted destructive read, then measure switched noise and writeback; the prior ideal six-range accuracy replay is not circuit evidence. Source-hashed transfer is in the destructive-read boundary report.

2026-10-05 shared read-cap layout: built/routed a five-10×10µm-MIM-per-rail bank (33.62×63.505µm, Magic DRC0, unique Netgen LVS,10 extracted MIMs). With diagnostic-grounded PEX substrate coupling, TT +750→+900mV read slope is0.11036mV/mV versus0.008246 direct, and0.10982 when two extracted TGs select the bank. Selector-off low range still produces symmetric±1mV outputs of about±3.392mV; selected high range gives−205.61/+212.18mV at±900mV. The high-range read is destructive. A conditional10µs precharge+10µs read serial schedule needs123,904 channels for30.976m states/5ms, and the bank bbox alone multiplies to264.54mm². Full read/write, noise/mismatch, calibration and actual service remain open. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json`.
2026-10-05: Continued full-float analog physical state/read qualification. Completed SS/0C and FF/85C selected high-range bank screens and added a 100us hold-time option to the bounded SPICE bench. FF +900mV rail programming failed the source guard despite ngspice completion; FF +750mV lost 7.49% of its initial differential even before read in 100us. Corrected the prior two-series-TG experiment by precharging its floating middle node; at FF +750mV it retained 1132.81mV differential after 5ms versus 1091.91mV for the single TG with the same MOS gate-cap, insufficient for this state cell. Updated the source-hashed shared-range report and active plan. Physical high-range MIM bank passes standalone DRC/LVS/PEX, but no combined state/sensor/writeback macro, full event sequence, original-float end-to-end accuracy, service closure, or full-chip layout is accepted. Next work: lower-leak state/access topology rather than further range-selector replication.
2026-10-05 isolation continuation: Schematic PDK 5V TGs with ideal off-gate bias -0.5/2.3V improve FF/85C +750mV five-millisecond differential hold to1282.61mV, from1200.15mV with 0/1.8V off gates; further bias -1.0/2.8V gives only1282.93mV. This does not solve small-cell retention and is not physically routed or reliability-qualified. Do not spend layout effort on the simple overdrive TG yet. Next isolate junction/parasitic leakage and compare a fundamentally different compact state/access organization against area and 5ms service.
2026-10-05 asymmetric isolation rejection: A schematic thick-oxide NMOS-only path on the low rail and PMOS-only path on the high rail, each with the same physical MOS gate-cap and ideal -1.0/2.8V off gates, ended at 1526.00mV differential from 1500.01mV initially for +750mV at FF/85C. At SS/0C the same high-range pairing ended at 1528.18mV. However the FF +1mV near-zero state started at 2.02mV differential and ended at 100.48mV after 5ms; the NMOS-only side drifts -49.45mV and PMOS-only side +49.02mV. Reject a fixed NMOS-low/PMOS-high pairing as a signed membrane store. This is a deterministic schematic diagnostic with extracted MIM/MOS gate-cap but no physical HV access cell, event injector, read/writeback, mismatch/noise or full-model validation. It does not justify a sign-dependent device selector because attaching both device types would restore their leakage paths. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-05 signed-polarity check: The same fixed asymmetric HV cell at FF/85C and reversed -750mV rails starts at -1499.94mV differential and ends at -1250.65mV after 5ms, while the positive state ends at +1526.00mV. This strong polarity dependence compounds its +100.48mV false near-zero state; no full signed transfer or calibrated inverse has been established. Continue treating it as a rejected fixed storage cell, not a signed PLIF candidate. Updated source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 open-PDK readiness recheck: The official ICsprout55 repository still lists SPICE models and RC extraction among unfinished deliverables (https://github.com/openecos-projects/icsprout55-pdk). The official OpenRPDK28 README still says its 28nm PDK is under construction (https://github.com/RIOSLaboratory/OpenRPDK28). IHP SG13G2 remains a real analog-capable 130nm preview PDK with MIM and MOS models (https://github.com/IHP-GmbH/IHP-Open-PDK), but its node and published capacitor density do not demonstrate a smaller resident state cell than the current SKY130 physical evidence. Therefore keep the SKY130 module bench and architecture gate; there is no newly source-backed ready small-node port that resolves the measured leakage/noise/service problem. This is a public-collateral screen as of this date, not a foundry capability claim.
2026-10-06 held-node leakage isolation: In the FF/85C +750mV, 5ms schematic 5V-TG screen, parking each overdriven off-switch source at its own initially commanded state instead of the fixed 0.9V shared bus leaves the 1V-overdriven TG low/high drift essentially unchanged: +141.6126/-75.4050mV on VCM and +141.6117/-75.4050mV parked. The NMOS-only and PMOS-only overdriven variants are likewise unchanged to the reported precision. By contrast, ordinary 0/1.8V off gates change the low-rail drift from +222.99mV to +12.71mV when parked. This separates a channel-voltage-sensitive component removed by off-gate overdrive from a remaining held-node leakage path; junction/body or model parasitics are plausible, but not proven without branch-current accounting. A fixed shared bus is the realistic comparison; parked per-state buses are diagnostic only. Do not pursue more simple gate overdrive. Next isolate transistor terminal currents and seek a storage node without a continuously reverse-biased diffusion before layout promotion. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 HV terminal-current diagnosis: Zero-volt probes were inserted at the body, off-bus and stored-node terminals of the schematic 5V TG without changing its FF/85C +750mV 5ms drift at reported precision. At 5ms the overdriven low-rail NMOS has body/source/state probe magnitudes 1.212/0.910/0.309pA, and the PMOS 2.426/0.910/1.512pA. On the high rail they are NMOS 2.494/0.910/1.581pA and PMOS 1.147/0.910/0.240pA. The signed body current agrees with the source-plus-state terminal currents within about 0.006pA for these devices. This directly locates the surviving pA path at the modeled body terminals, consistent with reverse-biased junction leakage; the gate terminal and transient capacitance are not independently probed. At the low stored node the PMOS state current overwhelms the opposing NMOS current; at the high node the NMOS dominates, explaining the 5ms compression. A viable compact store needs to remove or greatly reduce those continuously connected diffusion/body paths, not just add off-gate voltage. These are schematic 5V devices and diagnostic extracted MIM/MOS gate-cap, not a qualified physical HV cell. Source-hashed terminal values: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 diode state follow-up: Built a physical 1×1µm P+–Nwell diode primitive (DRC0, unique LVS, PEX, 3.65µm bbox) and screened direct two-diode MIM/MOS-gate-cap storage. The installed ngspice36 diode forward model gives unphysical femtoamp linear current and misleading near-perfect hold/no write; ngspice47 on the same PEX conducts and writes both signs, exposing identical broadcast disturbance in a second cell. The ngspice47 FF/85C 5ms physical-diode/well-proxy hold retains only 82.84% of the high-range differential and 86.2% near zero. Rejected the two-diode shared-bus resident state, preserved versioned reports, and kept full analog goal active. Next compare an actually addressable storage organization with correct ngspice47 forward/reverse models and quantify physical state area, retention, precision and service before any replication. No combined neuron macro or full-chip layout is accepted.
2026-10-06 simulator cross-check: Repeated the extracted 1/2µm TG plus small routed MIM state FF/85C 5ms hold in ngspice47. The stored 0.6/1.2V pair drifts to0.791701/1.08087V; the ideal-switch control does not drift. Updating the diode simulator alone does not rescue the compact MOS-access state. 2026-10-06 refresh check: Even a shortened100µs ngspice47 physical-diode/well-proxy hold loses5.878mV from a1.5V differential. This does not rescue the rejected direct diode shared-bus topology. Continue looking for an intrinsically lower-leak, addressable state or a physically justified architectural change; do not start full-chip replication.

Deprioritize linear storage expansion under this proxy. Conditional equilibrium kTC calculation for5uV actual differential linear-compute noise requires~395.6pF per rail at85C; published typical3.3V oxide density4.4fF/um2 gives bare two-rail plate repetition111.5mm2 for the ideal620-channel/100ns floor or1114.1mm2 for6196 channels/1us, excluding all contacts/access/encoding/settling. This is an analytic research estimate, not an implemented array or completed chip. Official density source: https://gf180mcu-pdk.readthedocs.io/en/latest/analog/spice/elec_specs/elec_specs_5_3.html . Reviewed oxide breakdown/LV electrical specs do not provide an operating gate-leakage bound on those pages; gate leakage remains unqualified. Evidence: results/gf180_phase_noise_read_boundary.json, gf180_write_noise_float_proxy_mapping.json, gf180_original_float_write_noise_boundary.json, scripts/characterize_gf180_gate_state_write_noise.py and scripts/summarize_gf180_float_noise_boundary.py. ## GF180 assembled reader: physical checks, settling and noise — 2026-10-06

Built the seven-MOS routed OTA core and grounded7/22um MIM primitives, then combined OTA, long-gate reset pair, reference diode and both caps into one10MOS/2MIM reader. Magic DRC0, Netgen unique LVS, no extraction feedback, real GDS; reader138x79.24um (10,935.12um2),50 capacitance entries. Fixed0.8V schematic bias had approximately28x corner current spread; a20uA external current into a physical bias diode gives approximately115..118uA total reader current. External current/reference supplies and phase sources are still ideal. Direct physical Miller capacitance is used without a provisional compensation resistor. MIM primitives now ground substrate explicitly; floating-VSUBS predecessor runs are superseded.

Combined capacitance-extracted read screens complete at typical27/FF85/SS0 and typical2mV offset. Gain from held state span to inverted output is0.42184/0.42814/0.41350 (near-zero typical0.42436). Maximum100ns-versus900ns output differences are0.761/3.338/0.383uV, while state is driven to approximately1.6493V and erased. Zero read output is1.6541..1.6547V and subsequent access switch-off changes output by millivolts. No physical reference subtraction, restore, encoder or complete neuron result is implied.

A naive read-phase DC operating point saturates output at0.021V and is invalid for transient noise referral. A separate bench freezes OUT/SUM to completed transient zero-state voltages using ideal1e18H AC-open auxiliary inductors, then characterizes the stationary small-signal noise. Output RMS over10Hz..100MHz is268.16/285.74/261.62uV; typical reset-on noise297.72uV. These are stationary phase screens, not sampled state error or covariance. Secant gain referral gives633..667uV linear input proxies, not decoder/model acceptance. Noise-current input referral is in amperes because the AC probe is a current source.

One/four separately extracted22x22um MIM output loads reduce typical output noise to235.80/201.83uV but increase100ns settling differences to33.08uV/21.58mV; four-load500ns difference is2.47uV. Increased output load alone is not selected as the accuracy solution. Ideal spectral boxcar/difference probes remain unimplemented; overlapping acquisition windows and same-phase noise correlation cannot establish distinct pre/post sampling cancellation. Next prototype physical averaging/correlated sensing and analog restore/nonlinear encoding; measure switched noise, covariance, mismatch, qualified gate leakage, resistance extraction, original-float accuracy, array density and complete5ms service. Full-float analog goal active, no QAT/digital fallback or full-chip completion. All launched runs terminal. Reproducible scripts: build_gf180_shared_ota_core.py, build_gf180_mim_cap.py, build_gf180_charge_read_frontend.py, screen_gf180_active_charge_read.py, characterize_gf180_charge_reader_noise.py, summarize_gf180_charge_reader.py. Source-hashed evidence: artifacts/analog_asic/results/gf180_combined_charge_reader_boundary.json.

## GF180 permanent-gate sensor alternative — 2026-10-06

Completed two source-follower/output-selection macros from official1/1um NF sensor,1/1um NF select and2/1um PF select PCells. Both have real26x18.04um GDS, Magic DRC0/unique Netgen LVS and no extraction feedback;18/17 extracted capacitance entries. Artifact suffix shielded is a route-variant name, not separate shielding qualification. A schematic follower with the earlier0.28um select moves held gate by roughly7mV at model zero, versus erasure by the charge reader. Layout parasitics increase disturbance: unshortened near-zero first-read post shift31.2mV, shortened rail with physical park about17.2mV at typical27C,5.14mV FF85 and20.28mV SS0. Near-zero paired span increases0.567/0.601/0.606percent after first read. The small grounded7um MIM bus load settles500ns-versus1900ns to within0.043uV in these deterministic near-zero screens. Three4us-spaced reads complete; three5ms-spaced reads also complete at all three corners, with another individual-gate141..431uV movement over the extra10ms. The latter includes hold drift and sensing history, not isolated read error. No weighted events, beta, threshold, adaptation or long-stream model result is included.

A stationary selected-read noise bench freezes DC membrane to the transient value through an ideal AC-open1e18H inductor and leaves the held gate floating in AC. Bus RMS over10Hz..100MHz is142.66/152.85/137.93uV typical/FF/SS; held-gate RMS6.697/7.274/6.490uV. Read current1uA, supplies and references are ideal/noiseless. These phase spectra do not establish sampled state error, inter-phase covariance or physical bias noise. Local sensor threshold mismatch/self-reference remains an essential next gate.

The permanently attached sensor cannot be treated as shared per processing channel. Its standalone469.04um2 guard/routing footprint repeated once per30,976,000 identities gives14,528.98mm2 for one stored rail, before storage and other circuitry; differential duplication doubles this term. This is an unoptimized repetition estimate, not an array density lower bound or chip result. Retain this sensor as a physical alternative to compare with shared-reader restore/encode; neither is accepted for full-chip assembly. Next characterize local mismatch/physical self-reference and array packing alongside actual analog nonlinear restore, switched noise, qualified gate leakage, original-float accuracy and complete5ms service. One earlier high-range unshortened/ideal-park read aborts at5.0025ms and remains incomplete; shortened/physical-park screens complete. All launched runs terminal. ## GF180 mismatch and physical sensor-referenced zero writer — 2026-10-06

Official per-instance fets_mm screening of the routed1/1um source follower/output-select macro at ideal gate1.648/1.650/1.652V yields32sensor zero-output sigma4.299/4.107/4.268mV for seeds17/101/2026. Mismatch-disabled control sigma0; gain mean0.77118, gain sigma approximately0.00022..0.00024. Model cards are unchanged, global variation disabled. setseed then reset gives identical seed17 parsed rows on independent invocation, persisted in gf180_sensor_mismatch_seed17_reproducibility.json. Subtracting each sample's own zero in this DC screen is a software oracle, not physical calibration.

A different bench physically closes one shared extracted OTA through the selected resident sensor and write TG, sequentially programming each gate until its own source follower reaches an ideal common1.2V setpoint. After write release and approximately5.09ms holding, three8slot typical27C mismatch runs give read-output sigma48.35/66.62/54.28uV. Program/released/read values show that most local sensor threshold offset is absorbed into the gate's own zero level; shared common output error remains7.95/9.18/11.13mV versus1.2V. This is initialization, not spike reset or a complete PLIF update. Any later event/beta/subtractive-reset operation must preserve the model semantics and use a qualified analog encoded-state transfer.

Built the shared writer as one12MOS/1MIM routed macro:138x80.04um,11,045.52um2,61 capacitance entries, real GDS, Magic DRC0/unique Netgen LVS/no extraction feedback. It includes OTA,1um complementary input feedback muxes, bias diode and grounded22um MIM direct compensation. Its8slot typical mismatch seed17 read sigma50.16uV, mean1.210813V, confirms reduction of local scatter but retains a10.813mV shared offset. State/sensor/load/park pieces remain separately extracted. The combined writer also completes FF85/SS0 at1.2V, with5ms read means1.208869/1.207214V, and typical1.1/1.3V commands yield1.108135/1.307552V. Neither PVT nor voltage transfer is calibrated/accepted. The initial eight-slot bench stopped before the final two measurements; correcting its horizon completed the campaign before using its results.

A typical4slot combined-writer energy screen integrates VDD over2us programming/read windows: average780.87/768.51pJ. These include quiescent writer/current-reference consumption; phase drivers, initial clamp, external setpoint/reference supplies, sampling/restore/encoding, array leakage and full-chip service are absent. The2us programming window is not a complete population-service measurement. The source-follower remains resident per stored rail; writer sharing does not resolve its array footprint.

Next build actual shared offset storage/subtraction and sampled analog restore, then nonlinear encoding and array-packed sensor comparison. Require switched noise/covariance, physical reference noise, PVT/range, local mismatch, qualified gate leakage, resistance extraction, original full-float accuracy and complete5ms service before acceptance/replication/full-chip layout. All launched runs terminal and full analog goal remains active, with no QAT/quantized/digital fallback.
